ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73076	Thank you, Mr. President. The Committee on Human Rights and the Rights of National Minorities discussed the proposal for a Law on the Protection of Persons with Mental Disorders at its 42nd session on 11 February 2014. The Committee considered the proposal of this law as an interested working body. In the debate, the committee members unanimously assessed that the proposed legal solutions represent significant progress in achieving and protecting the rights of persons with mental disorders. Further explanations of the proposed provisions relating above all to the application of specific medical procedures and biomedical research have been requested. As pointed out, people with mental disorders were most often forced into a psychiatric institution, and this circumstance certainly reduces the autonomy of a person, although Article 18 of the cumulative conditions for the use of electro... e.g. Treatment, inter alia, with written consent of persons with mental disorders, raises doubts about the autonomy of such consent to electroshocks. The reasons for those articles have also been requested to interpret the retention and accommodation of persons with mental disorders in a psychiatric institution based on the decision of only one person, not teamwork of experts. For example, Article 3 of point 12. On the basis of this article, people with mental disorders are forced to be detained until the court's decision and that this is based on the decision of psychiatrists. And Article 28 indicates that forced accommodation in a psychiatric institution is proposed by a doctor of medicine based on a manual. So a doctor of general practice, not a psychiatrist. The expectations are expressed that such proposed laws, legislative provisions will constitute a strong mechanism to prevent possible abuses of the Institute of Forced Accommodation with the aim of achieving certain political, property or personal benefits as has occurred in several cases that have been followed in public media over the past years. A significant contribution has been made to the debate by the Advocate General, the Advocate General for Children and the Deputy Advocate for Persons with Disabilities. The Ombudsman pointed out in particular that her office receives a large number of complaints from persons with mental disorders and their families, of course, and that they are also visited by psychiatric institutions as part of a national preventive mechanism to prevent torture and other cruel, inhuman or humiliating procedures. In order to further improve the proposed law, it proposed the legal regulation of the species, shrubs and conditions of application of the coercion measure to persons with mental disorders located in a psychiatric institution, as well as restrictions on the application of coercion measures to certain categories of persons with mental disorders, for example children. In addition, he suggested that this law apply to persons with mental disorders who are assigned to an investigative prison, not only to those who are in prison. In the end, as Article 26 is regulated by the obligation of the psychiatric institution to inform the Ombudsman of the accommodation without consent of the person with disabilities who will then verify the eligibility of such accommodation by the Deputy Advocate General of the person with disabilities has pointed out that they are in agreement with the proposed decision and have found that the provisions of this law, the legislative proposal, are significantly better than the applicable legal standards. Finally, it is proposed that provision 18, Article 18, be supplemented by a similar solution referred to in Article 19(3), so that we return to electroshocks, that electroconvulsive treatment be explained to the person with mental disorders in a precise and understandable way, unlike what many think these types of treatments are still used. The opinion is that the possibility of shortening the duration of forced detention of a person without a court solution should be considered which determines forced accommodation for a period shorter than the proposed 8 days. And after the debate, the Committee unanimously decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to draw the next conclusion. A proposal for a law on the protection of persons with mental disorders and attitudes, proposals and opinions presented in the proposal is adopted, in the debate on the draft law, the Proposer is referred to in order to prepare the final draft law. Thank you, Mr. President.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73078	I would like to thank the president, representatives of the ministry, colleagues and colleagues. So, before us is the proposal of the Law on the Protection of Persons with Mental Disturbation. We at the club, too, as fellow Furio said, believe this is a significant step in progress and the achievement and protection of the rights of persons with mental disorders. This law is in line with a number of conventions, so it is in line with the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, the Convention on the Rights of the Child, and the recommendations of the Council of Europe. And in any case, we believe that this bill is going towards reducing the stigmatisation of persons with mental disorders who are very much still expressed in our society. In fact, what is changing in this, specifically changing in this Law? So in this law is prescribed, carefully prescribes a way of protection and confidentiality of data of persons with mental disorders, complete medical records. The same careful prescription is given for the treatment, or for the procedures to be used, and should not be carried out for the purpose of treatment. It says that, therefore, psychosurgery and castration are forbidden, but is still allowed, so electroconvulsive therapy and that's what probably creates a lot of negative reactions. The fact is that this electroconvulsive therapy in the Republic of Croatia is being conducted in two other institutions, is not so common and represents one final treatment if, therefore, everything else is exhausted. What changes this law is a fact, therefore, that a person who submits to electroconvulsive therapy must give personal consent. So no longer can someone else sign for that person to whom electroconvulsive therapy will be applied. As for, therefore, further changes to this Law, means when we talk about the accommodation of patients in psychiatric institutions, so the accommodation of patients in psychiatric institutions can be given with consent, can be without consent and can be forced accommodation. What is the newspaper in this law is, therefore, accommodation without consent with the approval or signature of a confidential person. So this is where you introduce the concept of a person of trust. It's the paper of this law. So, at some point in our lives, each of us can choose a person of trust who, in the event of a disease, will decide whether or when this person is going to be treated, psychiatric treatment. The newspaper is also at forced accommodation, and we know that this has been said a lot lately. We read in the papers and abuses of such forced accommodation. What this law introduces is thus reducing the possibility of forced accommodation from 72 to 48 hours. And what we would like to point out at the club is, in any case, in Article 28. So at forced accommodation, it means that people who directly threaten their own or other lives, health or safety can be placed in forced accommodation. This is the case in Article 27, and Article 28 states that a person who has been forced into an institution may be on the basis of a physician’s medical instruction which has personally examined and written a prescribed document on that review. In the same article, paragraph 3 says without the medical instruction of doctors in particularly urgent cases of serious and direct endangerment of their own and other lives. So, as Article 28 refers to Article 27, where we have said that these are actually persons who are at risk of their own or others' lives, and what practice shows and fellow doctors will confirm, very rarely is called when the acute condition is psychiatric. Most of the time, you're called an ambulance and the police. So in this context, if the manual had to be written, it might happen that in the middle of the night you can't simply get a doctor's general medicine, you won't get a manual. And the question is then, given that it does specifically say emergency cases here, we might need to formulate ourselves a little bit better here because practice shows that in acute conditions it's basically called emergency medicine and police. Very, very rarely you are called a general medical doctor because if a patient is willing to reach a general physician accompanied by one of the families then it is actually just a worsening of the current disease and it is not such an aggressively acute emergency. I would also like to ask you to consider, if possible, the formulation of this Article 28. What is certainly more important to highlight and what is to praise is the specialization of judges since it is therefore very specific cases. Therefore, specialisation of the judges of the county courts is being introduced and, in the very context of this, the judge is no longer dependent solely on the opinion of a doctor, or psychiatrist, but can therefore also provide for further explanations and facts in order to make the best decision on this forced accommodation. It is also absolutely commendable, therefore, that in the trial of persons with mental disorders who have been tried most often in courtrooms without the presence of persons with mental disorders according to the proposal of this law, this is, therefore, exceptional in courts, and that means the trial must be conducted in psychiatric institutions with the presence of the person himself. Which is certainly progress and absolutely commendable. As well as the fact that a person with mental impairment as well as a legal representative can seek a second opinion, especially a person who does not work in an institution where he is treated directly. This is also important for the prominent. I would certainly like to emphasise the fact that the treatment of an innumerable person is being reintroduced. In any event, this applies to those persons where it has been found that a criminal offence cannot be repeated, i.e. that all the conditions are obtained for a person to be free from his or her treatment. This is certainly good in terms of rehabilitation and resociation and return to a normal life of people with mental disorders, which must actually be our goal to all of us. As far as the measures themselves are concerned, it is important to point out, therefore, that the Minister of Health is asked to prescribe the correct procedure and way in which and when it will be able to apply the measures of coercion and that they are in some way informed that they are simply equal in each institution because practice shows that this has not been the case until now. As well as the fact that social care institutions will no longer be able to implement coercion measures unless the institution is a working psychiatrist. And what I want to point out in the end is that this State Commission for the Protection of Persons with Mental Disturbation is transferring from the Ministry of Health to the Ministry of Justice and this is in line with what the SDP Club on the Ombudsman's report for people with disabilities has repeatedly pointed out. People with disabilities don't belong to the Ministry of Health, they actually belong to each of us and their needs are nothing different than any other person. And I want to point out and really commend it as a very good newspaper. What we still want to warn us about at the SDP club, which we need to take a lot of accounts for, is this working therapy. And we talked about it on the Health Committee. So today is the fact that psychiatric institutions are conducting work therapy. Work therapy is conducted in most cases that these patients, therefore, actually clean, feed other patients, change diapers, swim, shower, actually do work for people who are employed for these jobs within the hospitals themselves. In fact, the purpose of working therapy is not that, the purpose of working therapy has an exact purpose and goal and the goal is actually to get a person better, rehabilitate and return to normal life as soon as possible. In view of the fact that this bill is definitely aimed at reducing the stigmatisation and reduction, i.e. putting persons with mental disorders in one completely different picture in the society where it belongs, the SDP club will support this bill first reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73106	Dear President, colleagues and colleagues. In view of the need to bring legislation into line with international documents and the harmonisation of legislation and practices instead of new amendments and additions to the existing law, a new proposal for a Law on the Protection of Persons with Mental Disorders has been drawn up. The basic question is what the law represents essentially. It is clear that the possibility of applying one law depends not only on good wishes and strict adherence to the standards set in different conventions but also on the economic development, culture and tradition of the individual community. It must be pointed out that the legislator must be aware of the fact that a large part of the psychiatric patients are not considered sick or wanted to be treated, and that their current behaviour is not dangerous for them or for the environment, and therefore justifies the fear that the law will lead to a reduction in the total number of hospitalized patients. The proposed solutions would be good if there was a properly developed psychiatry in the community and a clear concern for society to help these people within their midst. This one, however, simply does not exist, or is not sufficient. Thus, the law which is some of the fundamentally declared objectives of human rights protection and health improvement could lead to a reduction in hospitalised patients, an increase in emergency reception, more aggressive patients, an increase in the need for forensic treatments and an increase in social cases. If that happens then the story of the protection of human rights will essentially turn into its own negativity. Part of our complaints is purely technical and we find it relatively easy to correct. For some of the solutions, although present in the current law, we believe that they would have opposite, therefore, harmful consequences. Article 3 of the bill determines the meaning... in this law. Paragraph 13 sets out what consent is. In practice, it means that a person who does not believe that she is sick, who knows that drugs are dangerous, and often really endangering, freely agrees to be taken away from her freedom, which is hard to believe. The problem should solve paragraph 16 which determines accommodation without consent as accommodation in a psychiatric institution of a person with serious mental disorders who is unable to give consent so that written consent is given instead by a person of trust or a legal representative. What, though, if there is no suitable person of trust or legal representative? A completely unnecessary provision of break 2, Article 8, which determines that non-adjustment to social norms should not in itself be a reason for setting a psychiatric diagnosis. Only what is present in the classification can be diagnosed, and not the adjustment to social norms is not a diagnostic category and is impossible to diagnose as such. It can only be a symptom within a clear diagnostic category. Article 10(2) lays down the accommodation of a child with mental disorders requiring treatment in a psychiatric institution and attributes the obligation of a psychiatric institution to ensure adequate accommodation if there is no department intended for the treatment of children separated from the adult ward. We believe that this should be the ministry's task, not psychiatric institutions, because it would certainly avoid the possible dangers of such prescribed obligations, such as this. that juveniles are admitted to the Department of Forensic Psychology, which is what happens in practice. Article 12(3) states that deprivement of a business capacity does not mean inability to grant consent. There's a great danger for a psychiatrist here. Let's assume that a psychiatrist at 2 a.m. urgently received a person of deprived business capacity who gave written consent. Then, after treatment, an unwanted outcome occurs, what happens when such a person consents to the procedure and then negative outcomes occur because of the procedure? In a possible future trial, it is very easy to prove that such a person was unable to give appropriate consent. Moreover, it is difficult to imagine that for example, a surgeon would operate on a person who was deprived of his professional ability without the consent of a guardian. Article 14 provides for the rights of persons with mental disorders. We stress the necessity of prescribing the right to written notices, not just oral, or the obligation of a psychiatric hospital to create a brochure or leaflet about the program of the institution and the rights of patients, which each patient would receive on the occasion of hospital reception, or department, as well as the family of patients. Patients who are unable to understand the brochure should receive interpretation according to their options. In subparagraph 5, paragraph 1 mentions resociation. Resociation is a term no longer used in modern psychiatric textbooks. Rehabilitation procedures are applied, so rehabilitation should be deleted because rehabilitation covers the meaning of this term. Article 28(3) sets out the need for psychiatric assistance in cases of serious mental disturbance. This necessary medical assistance can be refused by a psychiatric patient as well as by a person with diabetes mellitus or high blood pressure. The difference is that such a patient does not believe that he is ill, which means that some of the patients will only receive the necessary help if they are dangerous to others or themselves because they are currently not directly endangering their health, so that they cannot be forced to be hospitalized. One of the basic human categories is to take care of your health. In Article 56, paragraph 1 provides for the maximum duration of forced accommodation but the number of persons in a psychiatric institution. This article of the law has existed so far, and it exists in similar variants in many European countries. The strict adherence to this article puts a psychiatrist in a relatively favourable position because they are protected by law. In practice, however, a number of problems arise. The smaller problem is that sometimes the law doesn't define which is the longest penalty but only the smallest. The whole range of problems related to the release of such persons is far more important, and all are more or less related to the lack of adequate accommodation and supervision after the completion of the institutional treatment. First, there are patients who, although not at risk of life outside a psychiatric facility, would be practically impossible. Patients who do not directly endanger anyone but are highly unpredictable, virtually no one to communicate with, do not go outside of the department is dangerous. Then let's imagine that a schizophrenic patient after the murder was committed was well cured but believes he was cured and does not need therapy. He wants to go home and thinks he doesn't need future treatment and how and where to fire him if the locals don't want him. In chapter 8 of the coercion towards a person with severe mental disorders, there is a text that is mostly present in the applicable law. It is a fundamentally prescribed guideline in foreign literature, and we would like to say that these guidelines are not based on research but on good wishes and often on completely wrong assumptions. The law must clearly answer what is wrong with those patients who themselves require a slowdown or in our conditions of less isolation. Is it possible to use the restraint and what kind of administrative procedure must be followed by this kind of restraint? You won't find an answer to this question in foreign literature. This measure is often used after aggression in the psychiatric ward. But what about geriatric patients, some of whom regularly, and most often at night, fall into delinquent conditions? For them, the unrest and the getting up in the dark does not mean that they will attack someone, but that they will fall and break a part of the body. A broken hip in older people is often a death sentence. The law does not provide for such an indication, and it is very lifelike and present. Article 64(3) states "the application of measures of coercion will immediately be notified to a person of trust, a legal representative and a lawyer of a person with mental disorders". Although widely copied this item is completely inappropriate for a number of reasons. What does that mean right now? Does this mean that at three o'clock in the morning, they're going to inform a lawyer, and who's going to bear the costs of calling a lawyer, and the patient's ability to choose whether or not he wants to report this experience to anyone or an example of a schizophrenic patient who was aggressive in the department? It's very likely that a patient like this was aggressive at home as well. In this case, the message to a representative, lawyer or family member is not only because of the urgency of the coercion measure, but dominantly that the patient is aggressive and when he is in a protected environment, when under supervision and when he is taking therapy. The question is what it means for the patient to return home, and the return to social life is the goal of the treatment. In addition to the above, this item is extremely out of line with the rest of the law. These patients can decide whether they want to be treated, can vote in the elections, can read the newspaper, get visits, talk to everyone and every phone but cannot decide whether or not to report to anyone about their experience. We hope that the proposal will consider and respect our proposals and remarks when drawing up the final draft law.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73129	Thank you. We're continuing the debate individually. It's my dear colleague Mirela Holly's turn, please.
ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73130	Thank you, Vice President. At the beginning of the debate, I would like to say that I will support the proposal for a law on the protection of persons with mental disorders in the first reading, because I believe that this proposal brings a great positive step towards the protection of the rights of persons with mental disorders, and people with mental disorders are one of the most vulnerable and most discriminated against groups in Croatian society. I think it is necessary to defend this proposal from the assembly, because during the public debate, it became clear that there is a great resistance to this bill by the psychiatric profession. Why? Because this proposal introduces modern solutions that are in accordance with international law, which give greater rights to people with mental disorders, and restrict the space of freedom and powers that psychiatrists have so far. From the working group working on this bill, I received information that they were exposed to various indirect pressures so that the content of the legal proposal would return to the past. And that's not good practice. The most dissatisfaction that was not surprising was related to the scope of biomedical research and electroconvulsive therapy. In fact, the draft law tightens the conditions for conducting biomedical research, which certainly places a higher burden on the pharmaceutical industry, which sees its financial interest in such research. Furthermore, electroconvulsive therapy in the Republic of Croatia is currently being conducted in some medical research. These procedures are completely prohibited from agreeing to them by a legal representative, which means that only a person with mental disorders can make a decision about it. I believe that this caused the rebellion precisely because it was easier sometimes to obtain respondents for whom consent would be given by the guardian, and then the respondents did not have to be asked even when they opposed such a procedure. This has led and brought about because it is still possible for serious violations of human rights because there is really nothing to be done and to be asked about it. And that is why it is of great interest to adopt this Law as soon as possible. The legal text improves the provisions in accordance with the practice of the European Court of Human Rights in particular regarding persons with disabilities, the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and the Dignity of Human Beings as regards the application of biology and medicine, the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities and Other International Documents and the Law on Criminal Procedure. The lack of the law is that for persons with disabilities more flexible access is put in place and provides judicial protection only for those who oppose accommodation in a psychiatric institution according to the decision of the custodian. I believe that a person with deprived business capacity must also be asked for written consent for accommodation in a psychiatric institution, and if it is dangerous, then conduct a procedure for forced accommodation as well as for other persons who are not deprived of their business abilities. A compromise solution to introduce a special head that regulates accommodation without consent, as that ensures judicial protection for at least those who oppose accommodation in a psychiatric institution. Now I will also look back on some concrete articles of the bill that I find extremely important. Article 8(2) provides that non-adjustment to social norms should not in itself be a reason to make a diagnosis in accordance with the recommendations of the Council of Europe. That provision was opposed by a part of the psychiatrists, believing that only a diagnostic standard, that we had already prescribed a diagnostic standard when we stated that psychiatric diagnosis can only be made based on internationally recognised classifications of mental disorders. However, it is good that the provision is like this because it emphasizes that the environment itself must not be the reason for the diagnosis, by the fact that psychiatrists can camouflage social norms under other diagnosis between national classifications, so they have done so very recently in some cases of homosexuality, and there have been more examples in the past of such treatment of political dissidents. In Article 12(3) of the basic principle, it is introduced that, prior to each medical procedure, the ability of a person to consent to this procedure must be determined. Paragraph 3 provides that this capacity must be determined every time even in a person who has been deprived of his or her ability because it is a mental capacity that can vary. And it's absurd to be a person who, for example, is 10 years ago, the deprived of his business skills now considers him incapable of giving consent. In Article 19(1), (2) and (4), it is proposed to introduce stricter criteria for conducting biomedical research on persons with mental impairment and that authorisation for conducting research should be given by the Committee for the Protection of Persons with Mental Disorders, which puts additional protection against existing mechanisms. Secondly, for each individual subject, a positive opinion must be given to the Ethics Commission, which puts the individual benefit of the subjects under consideration so that research cannot be carried out over abstract but concrete persons. And thirdly, the ability to grant consent must be determined by another psychiatrist who is not an employee of the research facility designated by the Committee for the Protection of Persons with Mental Disturbation in order to minimise the consequences of conflicts of interest resulting from psychiatrists and researchers conducting research on their patients and, therefore, they may be somewhat valued by the doctor-patient relationship. These provisions have prompted recommendations as they make it difficult for the pharmaceutical industry to conduct research on people with mental disorders and, of course, it is also related to financial interests. Article 66 introduces the possibility of independent judicial supervision in the event of the application of a measure of coercion in psychiatric treatment when a person with mental disorders so requests. This is about the protection provided for in Article 3. Conventions on Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, Protection against Inhumane and Degrading Procedures. In chapter 9, a binding statement makes a brand-new model that has not been known in Croatian legislation, allowing each person to make a predetermined decision on how he wants to be treated in the application of psychiatric medical procedures as well as a person who will make certain decisions in his name, for example. form of power of attorney. Such a solution is present in Anglo-Saxon and continental legal systems, in most Western European countries and significantly improves respect for the rights of persons with mental disorders, given that they are placed on a disposition to make decisions on how they will be treated in case they lose their capacity to consent. A similar solution is provided for in the provisions of the Family Law proposal, which is soon to be read again, in line with Article 12. The Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, although not entirely because it is still being maintained. the concept of mental capacity that the cited convention does not know. In the public debate, there was a dispute over this provision since the psychiatric profession believes it is better to leave a person in such a situation to the Centre for Social Care, but this would again lead to human rights violations because the Centre for Social Care through the guardians can only make decisions if a person is limited by business capacity. In order to prevent unnecessary restrictions on business ability, a binding statement has been put in place because the decision on medical procedures of persons with mental disorders can be organised in advance without the need to involve public authorities. In accordance with the Conventions on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities of Persons with Mental Disabilities, they shall take precedence over decisions by state bodies when it comes to issues closely related to their personality. In the hope that as soon as we pass this law, I hope that no time will pass until the second reading.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73159	We move to the next item of the agenda, that is - Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Police Affairs and Powers, first reading, P.Z. ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73160	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Honorable MPs and parliamentary representatives. As a reason for the adoption of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Police Authorities, it was that, during the conduct of police officers under the current Law on Police Affairs and Powers, we saw certain indetermination of certain provisions thereof and the need to do so, for their different regulations and certain nomotechnical shortcomings in the legal text. The next reason was that on 15 December 2013, the law amending and supplementing the Law on criminal proceedings with which amendments and additions were primarily drawn up to implement the decision of the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Croatia. Number II 448 and, secondly, as the above amendments and additions to the Criminal Procedure Act are removed among other things, certain structural deficiencies in the normative arrangement of the effectiveness of the previous investigation process, it was also necessary to bring the Law on Police Affairs and Powers in line with the newly established legal framework adopted by these amendments and complemented by the Criminal Procedure Act. In this connection, the creation of amendments and additions to the Law on Police Affairs and Powers has been approached to remove, firstly, and to correct the observed irregularities, i.e. the content of the law has been no-technically improved. Under b, the law lays down provisions which comply with the latest amendments and additions of the Criminal Procedure Act. And the third, under c, added provisions aimed at introducing both different and stronger control over the application of certain police powers, in a way that will reconcile the need... for police supervision and the need for urgent and effective use of police powers so that police can respond to the requirements for effective crime prevention and the detection and processing of perpetrators of criminal offences, particularly the most difficult forms of criminal offences. As the Law on Police Affairs and Powers following the accession of the Republic of Croatia to the EU, notified in the European Commission and in the Council and in the European Central Bank, it was necessary to include in the law a provision on the transposition of the following framework decisions by this Law on the legal order of the Republic of Croatia and this Council Framework Decision of 1306 2002 on joint investigation teams and Council Framework Decision of 27 November 2008 on the protection of personal data processed in the framework of police and judicial cooperation in criminal cases. The concept of criminal research has been redefined, i.e. it has been expanded and adapted to the real and real frameworks of police conduct. Under this proposal, criminal research would cover those powers, measures and actions that police take when they reveal, within their jurisdiction, crimes and offences, a search for their perpetrators or finds and provides clues to what extent and reach the concept of consent with real criminal practice. In this way, so far, doubts about the overall and widespread application of police powers that are interdependent and unambiguous for full consideration of criminal research would be removed, regardless of the legal provisions of that power. In this regard, the results of the crimes may be considered an integral part of the criminal research which makes the proposal for a new resolution more clearly defined and the doubts that arise in practice. The paper also introduces the notion of analytical processing that allows the police to assess the justification for criminal research or to represent its component. It is also proposed to amend that provision in full, in order to address the real problems identified in the implementation of the provision of Article 7 of the relevant Law on the provision of assistance at the request of the court of the State lawyer, the body of the state administration or the legal person with public authority in the exercise of the actions of those parts of the assistance. According to the current formulation, the police are obliged to assist those bodies in carrying out the operation of those bodies. It should be pointed out that, as a rule, those bodies should carry out their actions on their own and should only ask the police for assistance in situations where they are reasonably expected to resist the conduct of their actions. This does not, of course, mean that the police will not always provide assistance, given that the provision of assistance and intervention is subject to the provisions of Article 6 of the relevant law. However, the applicant considers that the proposed formulation is that aid is to be provided if, when implementing the act, those bodies are reasonably expected to provide more precise resistance and ensures that the aid is not required in advance to carry out all the actions of those bodies, for example, different inspection controls which should nevertheless be carried out independently. In practice, the situation has been rarely recorded that individual inspection services have addressed the police expecting that by using their powers, the police will allow them to carry out inspection surveillance that, for example, the responsible person in the legal person is obstructing them. However, in most cases the prevention of inspection surveillance is prescribed as a separate offence and it would be right for inspection officers to submit the indictments and take all other available actions for which they are authorised. Furthermore, the adoption of the proposed amendments to Article 7 does not raise a general obligation for the police to assist courts prescribed by the Court Act, nor does the aid required by the provisions of the Law on Criminal Procedure in a broader sense be provided to the State's attorney. Furthermore, there have been cases of finding a suspicious object that poses a direct danger to the lives or health of people or a danger to property, which, for the sake of its properties, was not possible in a safe way to transfer it to another place where it would be destroyed. Therefore, these amendments and additions to the law are proposed to establish the authority of police officers to destroy such an item at the site of discovery if they cannot make it safe or in a safe way to transfer it to another place. The provisions on the verification of the establishment of telecommunications contacts, so-called procurement of communications lists, have been amended in full. For the sake of more precise terms, the name of this police authority itself has been changed and this authority is proposed to call for a verification of the establishment of electronic communication. There are clearly cases in which this authority can be applied, namely the prevention and detection of crimes for which it is prosecuted by official duty and by their perpetrators, the prevention of the danger of violence and the search for persons and objects. In addition, the power to determine the position of the communication device is prescribed, to identify the place where the persons who establish electronic communication and the device identification code are located. Extending the powers to determine the position of the communication device is necessary in order to apply the powers when searching for persons. Furthermore, it is proposed to prescribe which persons may grant written approval to carry out the verification of the establishment of electronic communication. Those persons are precisely defined according to their functions with a level not lower than the chief of police administrations or the person replacing it. Therefore, there is no possibility of transferring this power to other levels or persons. An exception is the possibility that authorisation may be given orally in urgent situations in order to prevent immediate danger or violence or to seek hastily for persons in which cases it must be written confirmed within 24 hours. As an important newspaper, it is required that the approval to verify the establishment of electronic communication should be based on the facts from which it is apparent that other actions could not or will not be able to achieve the objective of police work or would be linked to disproportionate difficulties. So it's basically a subsidiary application of this police authority. A police authority has also been defined to take undercover police actions in a way that is prescribed and ambushed in addition to observation, escorts and traps. In addition to the fact that these actions can be carried out subsidically in cases where other actions cannot achieve the objective of police work. A covert observation and escort may take 15 days with the possibility of extending them for another 15 days. A written order to carry out an escort and observation may only be issued by the Chief Director of the Police or by the person authorised to do so. And in the event of an extension of these actions, only the Chief Director of Police. It also provides for the possibility of giving oral orders to carry out these actions, with mandatory written confirmation no later than 24 hours from the date of issue. It is also prescribed that persons may issue an order to conduct covert police actions, traps and ambushes that may persist as long as there are reasons for their application. Finally, as a newspaper in order to enable effective control of their implementation, covert observation and escort actions are required to report to the competent state attorney and chief police director within 48 hours of their completion. Amendments to the provisions on the protection of police officers after the use of coercion funds have been prescribed that the Ministry of Interior will provide legal assistance to a police officer in a procedure that precedes the criminal proceedings, criminal proceedings and damages to police affairs carried out in accordance with the law. According to Article 98 of the current law, the Ministry of the Interior is obliged to provide free legal assistance to police officers, whether or not they acted or applied police powers legally or not, leading to a situation where legal aid to taxpayers was paid and persons reported for suspected war crimes. The Ministry of the Interior is obliged to pay legal aid to police officers when they are being prosecuted and led on suspicion that they have given away official secrets, received bribes or in some other way illegal but also criminally misused police powers. The same conditions for providing free legal assistance are also prescribed for citizens who provide police officers with assistance in their affairs. As a newspaper, it is prescribed that the MUP be given the necessary medical and psychosocial assistance to a police officer who uses firearms in his police work. Furthermore, these amendments and additions, in accordance with the Ombudsman's proposals for persons with disabilities in the applicable laws, include provisions that prescribe police treatment of persons with disabilities and persons deprived of their business capacity. These provisions aim to ensure additional safeguards, i.e. additional protection against the violation of the rights of these persons in the event of police conduct. An important paper that brings these amendments and the additions to the law is the introduction of civil oversight over the application of certain police powers and the verification of the establishment of electronic communications and hidden police actions. In this context, it is proposed to set up a Civil Surveillance Council over the application of certain police powers which may be exercised after the completion of the criminal investigation during which those powers have been applied. The Council would be empowered to request a comparison of the data available with the information provided by the Directorate of the Police on the applicable powers referred to in Article 68 when carrying out controls under its jurisdiction. The law. Such a comparison of data is necessary to exercise effective control over the exercise of powers, but in no case does not mean the introduction of the powers of the OTC Supervisory Council. It is also proposed that issues of importance for the work of the Council be governed by the Act of the Croatian Parliament on the proposal of the Committee on Human Rights and the Rights of National Minorities of the Croatian Parliament. Furthermore, the proposed provisions would prescribe the composition of the council in a way that makes it five representatives of citizens appointed and resolved by the Committee on Human Rights and the Rights of National Minorities of the Croatian Parliament and that a police officer of the Police Department participates in the work of the Council. Members of the council would perform the function as an honorary function. A member of the Council could not be a person who is not a citizen of the Republic of Croatia, who is a member of political parties, for whom there are security obstacles identified by a security check, who is convicted or against it shall be subject to a criminal offence for which he is prosecuted under official duty and who is convicted or prosecuted for a violation of the nature of violence or for a serious offence for which the sentence of prison is prescribed. Finally, the obligations of Council members to keep the confidentiality of the information they have learned by participating in the work of the Council have been established. The other proposed amendments and additions to the provisions on certain police powers have corrected certain perceived deficiencies or added content which has been found to be missing or no-technically corrected in practice. It is important to mention the provision of Article 3a relating to the recording of the conduct of police duties and the application of police powers which must be announced in advance, which should primarily protect citizens from the improper application of police powers, as well as police officers against unfounded complaints against their conduct and should also serve as an effective means of preventing corruption against criminal offences, etc., with a completely new increase in legality, one level of increasing lawfulness in handling and reducing the number of attacks against police officers. Finally, the amended Article 11a and the added Articles 11a to 11f comply with the provisions of the law with the amendments of the Criminal Procedure Act in the part of the conduct of the criminal investigation, the examination of criminal offences. Basic and professional conditions for police investigators have been laid down and a provision on the independence of criminal research has been introduced in cases where there are grounds for doubting that a police officer is the perpetrator or victim of a criminal offence. The proposer concludes that, in the proposed way, in particular the amendments and additions to Articles 68 and 80 of the applicable law and the introduction of civilian control over the application of certain police powers, an appropriate balance will be reached between the justified need of the police to apply those hidden measures in preventing and processing the most serious forms of criminal offences with legitimate public requirements for the exercise of legal control, the application of such measures by police officers. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73180	On behalf of the SDP representative club, colleague Franco Vidovic.
ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73181	I would like to thank the Vice President, the Honorable Deputy Minister, colleagues and fellow MPs. In front of us is the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Police Powers, Jobs and Powers. It is a proposal which, on the one hand, is in line with the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Criminal Procedure Act which entered into force on 15 December 2013. Next, the harmonisation and transposition of the Council Framework Decision of 13 June 2002 on joint investigation teams and Council Framework Decision of 27 November 2008 on the protection of personal data processed in the framework of police and judicial cooperation in criminal matters. In view of the nostrification of the Law on Police Affairs and Powers by the European Commission, the Council and the European Central Bank. What is most intrigued in this proposal are provisions aimed at introducing different and stronger control over the application of certain police powers, in a way that will reconcile the need and interest of the public to monitor the needs of the police for the urgent and effective application of powers and clearly all for the purpose of preventing crime and the detection and processing of perpetrators of criminal offences, in particular those most difficult. The concept of criminal research is redefined and expanded and now it covers all the powers, measures and actions that police are taking under the provisions of these and other laws when they reveal, within their jurisdiction, crimes and offences, that is, the search for their perpetrators, find and provide clues. The law also adds the concept of analytical processing, which involves the application of analytical techniques to determine the existence of a basis for doubt in the preparation or initiation of a criminal offence. This measure is primarily a tool to help make a decision on the initiation of criminal research, and can also be carried out during the course of criminal research itself. This set of measures enables coordination with the real and real frameworks of police conduct. In view of the practical experience so far in the conduct of police work when certain cases of overdoing or abuse of police powers, or police conduct caused complaints against the work of a police officer, whose alleged and factual state was neither confirmed nor disproved because of lack of evidence. This bill opens up the possibility of shooting police operations, with appropriate technical devices. It is also possible to collect recorded material in the MUP information system, and could be used as evidence in possible disputes. The special importance of this measure is the preventive effect on the possible corrupt behaviour of police officers, which in itself causes a rise in dignity and reputation of police services and ministries. This bill brings a paper on police assistance at the request of a court, a state lawyer, a state body or a legal person with public authority. If resistance is reasonably expected in the execution of their acts. A request for assistance and assistance must be submitted to the Chief of Police at least 8 days prior to the enforcement of the act and the Chief shall inform the applicant of the application for approval and the means of providing assistance or possibly the reasons for the refusal of the request no later than 3 days before the request for assistance. In practice, it has been common for police aid to be sought in the most banignant things where there was no premise of resistance. This was largely done by police officers, on the other hand, did not result in any positive effects but only increased costs for the state budget. This approach to police assistance is granted only in cases where there is a serious presumption of resistance to the execution of acts is justified and we at the SDP club fully support it. We also support the fact that the proposal of the Advocate General for Persons with Disabilities is respected in this proposal, then the new paragraph 3 is added in Article 14, which particularly stresses the effective protection of the rights of persons with disabilities in terms of ensuring real adaptation and support to such persons in the event of coalition treatment, taking into account the specificities of each individual damage to the person with disabilities. Since protection mechanisms for treating children and minors in Article 11 have already been provided. The proposal for a law has been amended and complemented to ensure similar protection mechanisms in the process of police treatment against persons with disabilities and persons with disabilities. According to this proposal, changes and additions to the police may, as appropriate and by assessment, engage for this purpose and professional assistance to persons outside the police system, but also those who are not experts but who have experience knowledge because they are close to persons with disabilities parents, guardians, sign language interpreters and the like. Article 82 is also proposed. It complements the law in such a way that the means of coercion are specifically applied with regard to the person with disabilities. In its letter of 12 August 2012, the Agency for the Protection of Personal Data pointed to the fact that the Regulation on the way police officers act prescribed that a police officer would resign, or to deny the provision of personal data at the request of a person whose right is the person whose data are being verified even though such a way of handling a police officer has so far not been based on the law. By adding a new sentence in Article 30, legal assumptions are created and given the right to a police officer that, in the event of a suspicion, data could be misused, in the event of a lack of information, to the person who requested the verification, it is suggested to address the competent unit of police by written request. It is also regulated to draw up documents on identity and ownership of things when a police officer collects information about a criminal offence carried out under official duty. Article 34 is complemented by a provision under which the written authorisation for the making of documents is exclusively issued by the Head of the Criminal Police Administration. This bill seeks to remove an administratively prescribed deadline within which a police officer may temporarily restrict access to space or movement in space and facility to the lives of persons or property. So far, this restriction has been the longest 12 hours, although in reality, the danger to people's lives or property has lasted longer. All we can think of are examples of fires, and then a series of uncontrolled explosions in the military "Old Guard" at Knin when explosives were activated for 3 days. This change allows the time limit of 12 hours to be replaced by the actual conditions in such a way that the restriction lasts as long as there is a real danger to the lives of people or to property, or until the danger is removed. It is also possible by this proposal when I have already mentioned the "Old Guard" that in the event of finding suspicious objects that pose a threat to the lives of people and the danger to property, which, because of their properties, is not possible in a safe way, to be transferred to another place where destruction would be destroyed at the very site of discovery. Article 66 provides for police authority to ensure the place of events. This article is complemented by paragraph 2 of point 5, which provides that a police officer who first arrived at the scene will take measures to protect and assist the victim. Namely, the assistance of victims under the obligation to protect victims who have already existed in the law implies police obligations to treat victims of all forms of crime by respecting the provisions of the Law on Criminal Procedure relating to the introduction of victims to their rights, the provisions of the Protocol on the Treatment of Family Violence, the Protocol on the Treatment of Sexual Violence, the UN Declaration on Fundamental Principles of Justice for Victims of Crimes and Power Abuse and the established best practice achieved in the European acquis. Since police are often the first to contact the victim, it is a special responsibility for police officers to identify which and what help it is necessary to provide or offer the victim. Article 40 relating to police powers of use of undercover police actions has also been amended. In addition to observation and escorts and traps, an ambush is now prescribed. Finally, the topic which is the biggest change this proposal amends and complements the Law on Police Affairs and Powers is borne by and in connection with the police authority to verify the establishment of a telecommunication contact in the public known as the Colloquial Name of the List of Communications. In view of the affair of codenamed "Juda" which has long been spinning in the media and is causing damage to the entire system where, if true, what is written about were witnesses of a real violation of police authority during 2011 when a list of electronic communications were reportedly carried out by the chief state attorney and editors of some newspapers in an attempt to reveal an unauthorized source of data to the media in connection with cases handled by the police and the DORH. Considering the so-called. Croatia's watergate affair, which we witnessed in 2012 when we tried to implicate the excess of police powers, and in the end it turned out that it was a silent attempt to protect the crime-mills we at the SDP club welcome any change that would contribute to clearer interpretation but also more transparent application of this power. In particular, we insist that this police authority is seriously affecting the lives of citizens in order to clearly establish rules when the authority is allowed to apply, who may authorise the application and to establish civil oversight over the application of this power. And we're thinking the same thing when it comes to police powers of observation and escorts. Therefore, Article 339a of the Criminal Procedure Act provides for the application of this measure in order to collect evidence according to the registered owner or user of a telecommunications asset while the application of this measure at the time of the detection of the perpetrators remained unregulated by that law. Therefore, in this proposal, the amendment and addition of the Law on Police Affairs and Powers and for its adaptation to the CAP proposed that this police authority can be applied to the prevention and detection of criminal offences that are being prosecuted by official duty and by their perpetrators, the prevention of addiction and violence and the search for persons and cases. In addition, the power to determine the position of the communication device, to identify the place where the persons who establish electronic communications are located, and to identify the device identification code is required. This extension of the powers particularly of determining the position of the communication device is necessary in order to search for missing persons. This proposal amending and supplementing the law has finally specified which persons can give written approval to carry out checks on the establishment of electronic communications and they cannot transfer their powers to other persons. Therefore, there is a clear responsibility for the application of powers affecting the lives of citizens. It is also proposed to amend Article 68 more precisely as regards the reasons for applying this power, which I have already talked about. It is proposed that this authority be subject to control by the Civil Surveillance Council with the authority of the escort and observation, which would, subject to precisely prescribed conditions, authorise the supervision of the establishment of electronic communications with clearly defined persons who can grant it to a significant extent exclude suspicion of the abuse of these police powers. And that's exactly what we at the SDP club are expecting and looking for. A clear system of application of powers that will be effective but not subject to abuse. On the other hand, we propose that, given the existence of the Civil Surveillance Council, the SOA not to form a new council but to extend the existing council's powers and numbers of members and to unite it with the existing powers and supervision of the powers to verify the establishment of electronic communications as well as hidden police actions. In fact, there is a practical reason for this and the definition of the Law on the Security Intelligence System of the Republic of Croatia, which has a Security Intelligence Agency Supervisory Board, is contained in Article 110, 111, 112, 113, 114 of that Act. By virtue of this law, the Council may oversee the powers of the prescribed law and the operation of the operational technical centre or of the OTC. The proposal to amend and supplement the Law on the exercise of police affairs and powers and the formation of councils in order to ensure that the purpose of its existence is not fulfilled, since the Council would, however, monitor the source of power, so whoever issued the warrant and the reasons for the application of the powers but would not have any legal authority over the operational technical centre, so it would be denied information on what happened to the warrant when it came to the OTC and when that order was carried out. As a result, we propose to the Proposer to reconsider the unification of all powers within the Civil Watch Councils, the security intelligence system to the team more so as to get a better control effect, remove or greatly reduce doubts about the abuse of those powers and the area of surveillance is very similar. The SDP club will support this bill in its first reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73226	We will discuss - Final bill on youth advice, second reading, PZ No. 457 Proposer of the Law is the Government of the Republic of Croatia. You've received the motion for an act. The provisions of the Rules of Procedure relating to second reading shall apply to the adoption of this law. The debate was conducted by the Committee on Law, the Committee on Family, Youth and Sports, the Committee on Local and Regional Self-Government. You received their reports at the session. The introduction of the bill will be explained by Deputy Minister of Social Policy and Young Mrs. Maja Sporish. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2014-03-05-0.u73227	Thank you very much, well-respected Vice President of the Croatian Parliament. Honorable MPs and MPs. The final proposal for a law on youth councils proposes to repeal the 2007 Youth Council Act. In fact, the current Law on Youth Tips over 6 years of application has shown a number of shortcomings, i.e. it has not fulfilled its basic function, which is to be the basis for young people to participate in decisions relating to young people at the level of units of local and regional self-government. The number of young people's established and functional advices, their work, co-operation with local and regional self-government units, and the real possibilities of consulting and affecting the decision-making process have not been achieved at a satisfactory level. According to local and regional self-government units, 19 of the 21 possible youth county councils have been established so far, 98 of the 127 possible city councils of young people and 143 of 429 possible municipal councils of young people. The data relate to those units of local and regional self-government where a decision has been taken to set up advice from young people, therefore only decisions, and not those in which the advice is established and active. The final draft of the law is the result of broad public debate and long-term consensus with stakeholders in the order of units of local and regional self-government, youth councils, youth associations and for young people and bodies of the state administration, all in order to promote the inclusion of the youth sector in making decisions of importance for young people and to seek to reach a broad consensus on the normative arrangement of this area. The final proposal for a law on youth councils responds to the perceived need to define more clearly the concept, role, goal and part-time advice of young people. The process of setting up and selecting members of the council is detailed, not involving the autonomy of local and regional self-government, but in a way to ensure a uniform standard of transparency and democratic principle. The catalogue of authorised candidates for members of youth councils has been organised and expanded to avoid discrimination against disorganised young people who are entitled to propose candidates for youth advice. The final bill defines the relationship between youth councils and units of local and regional self-government. The proposal regulates the participation of representatives of youth councils in the meetings of the representative body, the delivery of material for meetings and insight into discussions and the obligation to hold joint meetings of the representative body with the president of youth councils and to discuss topics and issues of importance and interest for young people. The proposal allows young people's advice to initiate decisions and to give opinions, proposals and recommendations to the representative body of their local and regional self-government units. Furthermore, the final draft of the law defines a way of providing financial resources for the work of youth councils and a minimum standard of the content of the Youth Council's work programme and its connection with the financial plan as well as the mechanism for mutual reporting of youth advice and units of local and regional self-government. In this way, the work of the council shall be directed and structured and official two-way communication shall be established between the units of local and regional self-government and the advice of young people. The proposed law controls the implementation of the law shall be placed in the power of the ministry responsible for young people. Using the management of the Youth Councils database, collecting information on the functioning of the advice, publishing annual reports and making recommendations to better functioning tips. During the debate on the draft law, certain observations were made which were considered and incorporated in the final proposal for a law. Among other things, the final draft of the law leaves the possibility for the Statute of the City of Zagreb to provide for the establishment of a Youth Council as advisory bodies of the City Council of Zagreb and also to define that the Council of the Young City of Zagreb, unlike the advice of young other units of the regional self-government, has at least 15 and not more than 21 members. This way special status is valued which the City of Zagreb has in its current regulations governing the area of local and regional self-government as well as the fact that the city of Zagreb far exceeds the number of inhabitants of each single county in the Republic of Croatia. Furthermore, provisions have been removed from the law which have been prescribed that the representative body will relieve a member of the Council of Youth before the term of office expires if he is given an unconditional sentence of imprisonment by a court of law. In view of the fact that such a reason for the resolution does not exist in relation to members of the representative body, and since impunity is not prescribed as a condition for membership in the Youth Council, there is no basis for the same regulation as the reason for the resolution of members of the Youth Council. In addition, a provision on disbanding the Council of Young People in the event of their inactivity, i.e. not holding a session for more than six months, is laid down by law. Clearer defined provisions on the cost of participation in the work of the Youth Council in such a way that it is prescribed that members of the Youth Council shall have the right to compensation for the transport costs for coming to meetings as well as other travel expenses directly related to the work and membership of the Youth Council in accordance with the decision establishing the Youth Council. The adoption of the final proposal for a Law on Youth Councils will take a step forward in regulating consultation with young people at local and regional level in the Republic of Croatia, and will allow for the establishment and better functioning of the Youth Council and those units that have failed to set up a Youth Council in the past period and ensure its smooth and successful functioning. The law will provide mechanisms for the participation of the Youth Council at the meetings of representative bodies of units of local and regional self-government as well as two-way communication and cooperation between representative bodies and their Youth Councils on issues of importance for young people. Youth advice is a form of active participation of young people in communities at levels where many decisions are made that directly affect people's lives. The active participation of young people is described in the documents of the European Union and the Council of Europe, and the democratic principles and standards of consultation that Croatia has adopted through the Constitution and numerous laws require us to guarantee a quality framework for consulting young people at local and regional level. So I urge you to support this bill. Thank you very much.

It is about the inability to hire, the inability to achieve existence, and then, of course, the establishment of a family and, in general, any other advancement. I'd say the young are living on a historic paradox today. Namely, after obtaining a degree and inability to hire, they continue their education. So they're willing to still invest in themselves, not waste useless time. So to gain additional knowledge and the highest possible competences, master's degree, doctorate, and again and after all, I can't get a job. And that's actually the paradox. The harder it is for young people to get a job today, the harder it is to hire for a very simple reason simply no jobs. The young are actually one huge dead capital, the capital in which we all invested, and so today we can't use it. Of course, it's not a choice of young people. They don't really have a choice right now. On the other hand, we are witnessing the lack of interest of young people in politics and then of engagement in the authorities. Young people do not recognize political engagement as a tool for solving their problems, i.e. as a tool for affecting their status. Youths need politics and politics need young people, politics needs creativity and youth enthusiasm. On the other hand, young people should be involved in the activities of representative authorities. They should simply not be concerned about who and how they decide to spend budget public money, which projects are realised, which are not, what needs are being met and which are not, where it is in this meeting the interests of young people. One word to young people must not matter how they decide their future. When we first discussed the Law on Youth Tips, we agreed that they were a good model for involving young people in the public life of local communities, but still insufficiently recognized by young people themselves. And we have to say, and this is in these discussions, in fact on several occasions, it has been pointed out that even local self-government has not broken up much of an effort to initiate the establishment of a Youth Council. Namely, as we have heard, neither the county nor the day today have yet to have established advice from young people. In cities, however, the worst situation is definitely in the lowest forms of local self-government, so in municipalities. Of the 429 municipalities of only 143 have established Youth Councils and definitely regardless of these specificities spoken by fellow Shoja, therefore we cannot say that there are some huge reasons why Youth Tips are not established. So, first of all, they were not founded because there was no real political will to establish them. Unfortunately, and where they are founded, the advice of young people is often a dead letter on paper, too often due to the poor relationship of local leaders to them, so according to the Youth Councils, for which young people were sooner or later rescinded, even the disappointed gave up every engagement. I'd say as in a number of other cases, we're adults. We have not worked hard enough to help young people, to direct them, and to convince them of the need to engage in the public life of local communities. Therefore, it is good that this final draft of the law includes a series of remarks and proposals that significantly alter this relationship, so the relationship between local self-government, local authorities towards youth councils, makes this relationship more quality, I would say more serious and attractive. Now this is far greater than the previous relationship based on the principles of cooperation or as the deputy said nicely on the principles of partnership with the provision of political, administrative and financial support. Better cooperation and political support will be achieved primarily through clearer provisions on how the representatives of the Youth Council participate in the work of local self-government representative bodies. Namely, the President, Vice-President or other member of the Youth Council will have the right and duty to attend the meetings of the representative body, with him not being a passive observer but having the right to participate in the debate without, of course, the right to vote. The co-operation also strengthens the provisions under which the municipal chief, mayor and mayor will hold joint meetings with the Youth Councils to discuss issues of interest for young people. These meetings will be held as necessary, at least every three months. Moreover, every six months local leaders will inform the Youth Council on their activities, which are important and interesting to young people. The Ministry of Social Policy and Youth also receives certain obligations. In fact, it will publish annual reports on the implementation of the Law on Youth Councils on its web pages, but it will also submit these reports to the boards of the Croatian Assembly responsible for young people as well as local, or regional self-government. What is important for this mutual relationship between local authorities and the Youth Council is the fact that the Councils of Youth through this final legal solution receive certain obligations. Namely, they will have to hold regular sessions, at least once every three months, and more often if necessary. For the first time, the law also included a proposal on disbanding the Council of Youths in the event of their inactivity with a clear notion of inactivity, which is inactivity. Therefore, the advice is inactive if it has not met or held a session for more than 6 months. Much clearer now is defined in this final proposal and financial support to the advice of young people. In fact, the law stipulates that members of the Youth Council are entitled to compensation for transport costs for coming to meetings and other travel expenses related to engagement in the Youth Council. This is important and this is interesting since members of the Youth Council are mostly students and other young people who do not have permanent monetary incomes. In the end, I would say that I believe that all these changes will act encouragingly on young people, that, with these provisions, the advice of young people will indeed become one quality, advisory mechanism to the representatives of the authorities in the process of making political decisions and will contribute far more than ever to the equal involvement of young people in addressing their needs and interests in local communities. Thank you.
